ful and kind to men. The angels are, I say, our allies as we fight against 
hostile powers, and in their number is [266] the Archangel Michael, who 
fought the devil for the body of Moses, and withstood the prince of the 
Persians in protection of the people’s liberty. For when by God’s command 
they had obtained control of all creation and were in authority over the 
mightiest of the nations, he judged Gabriel worthy to oversee the Persians, 
but bade Michael guard the people of circumcision. But according to some 
ineffable divine principle, one angel obliged the Jews to serve the Persians, 
while the other drew them toward liberty, and constantly inspired their 
worship of God, as he said, ‘Oh Lord omnipotent, how long wilt thou not 
pity Jerusalem and the cities of Judah, which thou hast seen these eighty 
years?’ In his vision Daniel saw him in flight, and his face was as the 
lightning and his eyes like torches of fire, and his arms and legs like the sheen 
of bronze, and the voice of his words like the voice of a mighty throng. It 
was one of them who turned aside Balaam’s ass, and saved him from impure 
enchantment. It was one of them who drew his sword before Joshua, son of 
Nun, and by his appearance urged him to engage his foes. It was one of 
their number who slew 180,000 Assyrians in a single night, and mixed with 
death the slumber of the barbarians. [267] One of them it was who lifted 
up the Prophet Habakkuk with a leap through the air, that he might save 
the Prophet Daniel among the lions. It is these and such as these who excel 
against the foe. Such was the divine Raphael who by cutting up the en- 
trails of a fish, cured a girl possessed of devils, and restored the aged blind 
man’s sight. Are not these who save one life worthy of great honors? Angels 
are not only set as guardians of the Gentiles, according to the saying 
(Deut., xxxii): ‘When the most high divided to the nations their inherit- 
ance, when he separated the sons of Adam, he set the bounds of the people 
according to the number of the angels of God;’ but also to every faithful man 
is an angel appointed. For when the girl Rhoda announced to the apostles 
that Peter stood before the door, when he had escaped from Herod’s prison, 
they said, in their lack of faith, ‘It is his angel.’ Our Lord bears witness to 
these things in his words: ‘Take heed that ye despise not one of these little 
ones; for I say unto you, That in heaven their angels do always behold 
the face of our Father which is in heaven.’ (Matthew, xviii, 10) Indeed, 
Christ set angels as guards over every church, even as John says in Revela- 
tion: ‘Say to the angel of the church in Smyrna, “I have seen thy poverty 
and thy affliction; but rich art thou (A poc., ii, 8,9).” Justly therefore do 
we honor the angels who love us and pray the Lord in our behalf. For they 
are, in the words of the apostle, ‘ministering spirits, set forth to minister 
for them who shall be heirs of [268] salvation,’ (Heb., i, 14) whose cham- 
pions and allies they are. You have just heard from Daniel how he brought 
the Archangel Michael, who does battle for our liberty before all men. Now 
one angel compelled the people to serve the Persians, as has been said; but 
the other was zealous to relieve their servitude. Thus Michael overcame his 
adversary; for the Jews, after crossing the Euphrates, received their homes 
from his hand once more, and rebuilt the city and the temple.” Likewise the 


318 Appendices 


great Epiphanius! said, “Holy writ declares that an angel is appointed to 
every nation. For an angel spoke to Daniel mentioning the Prince of the 
Persians and the Prince of the Greeks, as well as Michael the lord of the 
Persians. Moses has said, ‘He has set the bounds of the nations according 
to the number of the angels of God.’”’ And further, Hippolytus says, in his 
interpretation of Daniel: ? ‘‘‘In the third year of Cyrus the king, I, Daniel, 
was in mourning for three weeks, and in the first month I was consoled, after 
praying to God for twenty-one days, entreating of him revelation of a secret. 
And giving ear, the Father sent forth his word, and commanded that his de- 
sire should be fulfilled.’ And Daniel was beside a great river; a fair vision 
was to appear there, where God was desirous of remitting human sin. And 
he lifted up his eyes, and saw a man in purple; in his first vision he had be- 
held the angel Gabriel in flight, but this time he beheld the Lord himself, not 
entirely as a man, but appearing in human form, as he says, ‘Behold a cer- 
tain man clothed in linen, whose loins were girded in pure gold; his body 
[269] also was like the beryl, and his face as the lightning, and his eyes as 
lamps of fire, and his legs and shoulders like to pure brass, and his voice as of 
a great multitude. And I fell on the earth, and behold, he appeared unto me 
like a human hand, and lifted me to my knee, and said to me, “Fear not, 
Daniel; knowest thou wherefore I have come to thee? I will fight with the 
prince of Persia. But I will show thee the scripture of truth, and no one can 
vie with me in these things but Michael your prince. Him I have left among 
you from the day when you were zealous in prayer before your God, who has 
heard your petition, and I am come forth to do battle with the Prince of the 
Persians. There was some council not to let the people go, but I opposed it, 
and left here Michael your prince.”’’ Who is Michael but the angel ap- 
pointed for the people? As Moses says: ‘I will not go up in the midst of 
thee, for thou art a stiff-necked people, but my angel shall go with you 
(Ex., xxxiii, 2-3).’”’ 

Thus with God’s aid, through the supplications of the Virgin and his holy 
angels, the Russian princes returned homeward with great glory among their 
people and among all foreign nations, the Greeks, the Hungarians, the Poles 
and the Czechs, whence their fame penetrated even to Rome, to the glory of 
God, now, henceforth, and forever more, Amen. 

On October 7 [270] of the same year died the Princess, wife of Vsevolod, 
and she was buried in the monastery of St Andrew. On November 23 of 
this year died John, Bishop of Chernigov.

and on May 12 was betrothed to the daughter of Mstislav (she who was 
granddaughter of Vladimir) and she was brought to him on June 29. In the 
same year, Vladimir’s daughter Euthymia was married to the King of

Hungary. On May 25 of this year, David son of Igor passed away, and on 
the 29th was buried at Klov in the church of the Virgin of Blachernae. 
On November 3 of this year died Yanka, Vladimir’s sister and the daughter 
of Vsevolod, and she was laid to rest in the church of St Andrew which her 
father had built. As a maiden, she had also taken the veil in this same church. 
Toward the close of this year, on January 12, Theoktistos, prior of the Crypt 
Monastery, was appointed Bishop of Chernigov, and consecrated on the 
19th to the great joy of Prince David and his consort, for he had been the 
Princess’s father confessor. The nobles and all the people rejoiced, for his 
predecessor had been precluded from active ministration by an illness of 
twenty-four years’ duration, so that the Prince and his people desired a more 
active prelate, and thus were happy as they glorified God. Under the cir- 
cumstances, since the Crypt Monastety was without a prior, the brethren 
all met together and after electing [271] as their prior Prokhor the priest’s 
son, they reported their action to the bishop and Prince Syvatopolk, who 
gladly commanded the metropolitan to consecrate him, and he was thus con- 
secrated on Thursday February 9g, in the week before Lent; thus the com- 
munity entered upon the fast duly provided with a prior. 

662z (1113). On March roth, being the 29th day of the moon, and at the 
first hour of the day, there was a generally visible portent in the sun, which 
was reduced to small size, with horns at the ends like the moon. These 
portents forbode no good; such signs in the sun, moon, or stars do not apply 
to the whole world, but are visible only in the country where they appear. 
Thus in the days of Antiochus, there were portents at Jerusalem with armed 
warriors riding on steeds through the air amid the clash of arms. This was 
visible, however, in Jerusalem only, but did not appear elsewhere. Thus 
there was a portent in the sun which presaged the death of Svyatopolk. For 
afterward came the Easter festival, which was duly celebrated, but after the 
holiday the prince fell ill. Thus on April 16, at Vyshegorod, the pious prince 
Michael, called Svyatopolk, passed away. He was brought on a boat to 
Kiev, where they adorned his body and laid it on a sledge. After the nobles 
and his retinue had wept over him, chanting the usual [272] hymns, they 
laid him to rest in the church of St Michael, which he himself had built. 
The Princess his wife distributed many riches to monasteries and priests and 
poor, arousing universal wonder that any one could give so many alms. On 
the morrow, being April 17, the inhabitants of Kiev took council, and in- 
vited Vladimir to ascend the ancestral throne. On hearing their message, 
Vladimir wept, and refused to accept, mourning for his brother. But the 
men of Kiev plundered the palace of Putyata the Thousandman, and at- 
tacked and robbed the Jews. The citizens of Kiev once more sent mes- 
sengers to Vladimir saying, ‘Come, oh Prince, to Kiev; and if you do not 
come, know that much evil will befall. For it will not be merely a matter of 
robbing the palace of Putyata, or the hundredmen, or even the Jews, but 
they will attack your sister-in-law, the nobles, and the monasteries, and if 
they plunder the monasteries, you will be responsible for it.” When Vladi- 
mir heard these sayings, he went to Kiev.

The beginning of the principate of Vladimir, son of Vsevolod. Vladimir 
arrived at Kiev on Monday, and was met with great honor by the Metropol- 
itan Nicephorus, the bishops, and all the inhabitants. He thus assumed the 
throne of his father and his ancestors, and all the people rejoiced, and the 
rioting was quelled. When the Polovcians learned of the death of Svyato- 
polk, they gathered their forces and advanced to the Vyr. Vladimir [273 ] 
assembled his sons and nephews, set out for the Vyr, and joined with Oleg, 
but the Polovcians fled. In the same year, he located his son Svyatoslav in 
Pereyaslavl, and Vyachislav in Smolensk. On September 14 of this year 
died the prior of the Monastery of St Lazarus, a recluse of pious life, who 
passed sixty years in monastic orders and was ninety-two years old at his 
death. On September 11 of this year, Vladimir accepted the daughter of 
Volodar as wife for his son Roman. In this year, Izyaslav founded at Nov- 
gorod the stone church of St Nicholas in the princes’ palace by the market- 
place. In this same year, Vladimir placed his son Yaropolk in Pereyaslavl. 
In the same year Daniel was appointed Bishop of Yuryev and Nikita at 
Bvelgorod.
